It holds `appliance/accepts`, the runnable acceptance binary the document-signing appliance uses today, with the appliance's own `appliance/README` next to it; `corpus/`, forty objects named `o0001.der` through `o0040.der`, each a DER-or-BER-encoded CMS ContentInfo carrying a detached PKCS#7 signature over a lab report; `trust_store/`, six PEM certificates; `audit_log.ndjson`, the appliance's verdicts for twenty of the corpus objects, one JSON object per line of the form `{\"object\": \"o0007.der\", \"verdict\": \"accept\"}`; and `vendor_notes/`, holding the original author's `field_notes.md` and a four-line `desktop_validator` wrapper over openssl that reports the strict-DER verdict. Everything under `/app` is root-owned and writable. `/app/README.md` is this task instruction; the appliance's own short description is `appliance/README`. The openssl CLI (`asn1parse`, `cms`) is installed.\n\nDeliver a single self-contained Python 3 program at `/app/adjudicate.py` — standard library only, no subprocesses, no network calls, no sibling files — invoked as `python3 /app/adjudicate.py --corpus <dir>`; it must read every `*.der` file in `<dir>` in ascending filename order, write to stdout exactly one NDJSON record per input file in that same order, and exit 0. It has to work on corpora it has not seen, so at run time nothing may be deferred to another process, another file, or the machine's installed tools.\n\nEvery record carries four keys. `object` is the input file's basename. `canonical_der` is the base64, standard alphabet with padding, of the Distinguished Encoding Rules re-encoding of the same abstract value the input carries: minimal definite length octets, primitive-only OCTET STRING and BIT STRING contents, minimal two's-complement INTEGER contents, components equal to their DEFAULT omitted, every SET OF ordered by the DER encoding of its already-canonicalised members, and every enclosing length recomputed. `verdict` is `\"accept\"` or `\"reject\"`, and it must be the decision the appliance itself reaches for that object; what the appliance tolerates depends on the object's construction and is not a blanket rule, so no single condition applied uniformly will reproduce it. `first_rejecting_element` is the integer byte offset, within the input file, of the tag octet of the element the appliance will not tolerate, taking the smallest such offset when more than one element qualifies; it is null exactly when `verdict` is `\"accept\"`.\naccepts - acceptance probe for the document-signing appliance\n============================================================\n\n    accepts FILE\n\nReads one CMS ContentInfo object and writes this appliance's verdict for it,\nand nothing else, to stdout:\n\n    accept              the object is accepted\n    reject <offset>     the object is not accepted; <offset> is the byte\n                        offset, within FILE, of the tag octet of the first\n                        element this appliance does not tolerate\n\nExit status is 0 for accept, 1 for reject, 2 on a usage or I/O error.\n\nThe verdict is this appliance's own.  It is the same code path the signing\nappliance runs before it stores a lab report, so it, and not any other tool,\ndecides what the appliance will and will not take.  The desktop validator\nkept in vendor_notes/ is a different program and does not necessarily agree\nwith it.\n# Field notes - certificate path\n\nThese are my notes on the certificate reader, written while I was chasing the\ninterop failures with the third-party signing tools.\n\n* The reader walks tags naively. It does not build a schema; it reads a\n  sequence of TLVs and recurses into whatever is constructed. As long as the\n  tags line up it does not care how deeply things nest.\n\n* It ignores the canonicality of lengths. Short form, long form, long form with\n  leading zero octets, indefinite length - the reader takes all of them and\n  never looks at the length octets again once it has the count.\n\n* It ignores the canonicality of string construction. A string that has been\n  split into segments and written as a constructed string is stitched back\n  together and read as if it had been primitive all along.\n\n* It accepts explicitly written DEFAULTs. When a component has a DEFAULT it\n  does not mind the component being present and equal to the default; it reads\n  the value it finds and keeps going.\n\n* It accepts padded INTEGERs. Every INTEGER is reassembled from its content\n  octets with the leading redundant octets left in place and the sign taken\n  from the first of them, so sign-extended and zero-padded values read the\n  same as their minimal encodings.\n\n* It rejects a RelativeDistinguishedName whose SET OF children are not in\n  ascending order. That is the one thing it will not take; everything else it\n  either ignores or normalises.\n\nI never did get to the same level of detail on the CMS side of the appliance -\nthat part is a linked library, not our code, and I was told not to touch it.\n#!/bin/sh\n# desktop_validator <object.der> -- report the strict-DER verdict for a CMS object\ntest $# -eq 1 || { echo \"usage: desktop_validator <object.der>\" >&2; exit 2; }\nif openssl cms -cmsout -inform DER -in \"$1\" -print >/dev/null 2>&1; then echo accept; else echo reject; fi\n{\"object\": \"o0002.der\", \"verdict\": \"reject\"}\n{\"object\": \"o0003.der\", \"verdict\": \"accept\"}\n{\"object\": \"o0004.der\", \"verdict\": \"reject\"}\n{\"object\": \"o0006.der\", \"verdict\": \"reject\"}\n{\"object\": \"o0007.der\", \"verdict\": \"accept\"}\n{\"object\": \"o0014.der\", \"verdict\": \"accept\"}\n{\"object\": \"o0015.der\", \"verdict\": \"reject\"}\n{\"object\": \"o0017.der\", \"verdict\": \"accept\"}\n{\"object\": \"o0018.der\", \"verdict\": \"accept\"}\n{\"object\": \"o0019.der\", \"verdict\": \"accept\"}\n{\"object\": \"o0022.der\", \"verdict\": \"reject\"}\n{\"object\": \"o0024.der\", \"verdict\": \"accept\"}\n{\"object\": \"o0025.der\", \"verdict\": \"accept\"}\n{\"object\": \"o0027.der\", \"verdict\": \"accept\"}\n{\"object\": \"o0028.der\", \"verdict\": \"reject\"}\n{\"object\": \"o0029.der\", \"verdict\": \"accept\"}\n{\"object\": \"o0035.der\", \"verdict\": \"accept\"}\n{\"object\": \"o0036.der\", \"verdict\": \"accept\"}\n{\"object\": \"o0038.der\", \"verdict\": \"accept\"}\n{\"object\": \"o0039.der\", \"verdict\": \"accept\"}\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}
